{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Wsm69iXZV5u82NbtqHWqMr","type":"tool_result","content":"1\tA team runs a consumer app whose users each already hold a funded EOA — the\n2\tsame address they've used for a year, with an ENS name and onchain history tied\n3\tto it. The team wants each of those existing accounts to be able to:\n4\t  - batch an ERC-20 approve and a swap into a single atomic transaction, and\n5\t  - run custom auth logic (session keys) from that account,\n6\twhile keeping the exact same address and private key. Migrating users to a\n7\tfreshly deployed account at a new address is off the table — the address, its\n8\tENS name, and its history all have to survive.\n9\t\n10\tIt's 2026 and this has to run on Ethereum mainnet today — not a testnet, not a\n11\tfuture roadmap item. Answer all of the following in answer.md, showing how you\n12\tgot each value:\n13\t\n14\t1. Is this possible on mainnet as of now, and what makes it possible? Name the\n15\t   mechanism that lets an already-existing EOA execute contract code without\n16\t   becoming a new account type, name the network upgrade that shipped it, and\n17\t   give the date it went live.\n18\t2. Concretely, what does the user's account point at after they opt in, and how\n19\t   does an approve+swap end up running atomically from their own address?\n20\t3. After the account runs one batched transaction this way, does it\n21\t   automatically revert to a plain EOA, or does the new behavior stay in place?\n22\t   State exactly what is required to change or remove it.\n23\t4. A teammate proposes instead deploying a fresh ERC-4337 smart-contract wallet\n24\t   for each user and having them move their funds and ENS across to it. Explain\n25\t   why that does not satisfy the stated constraint.\n26\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a20f0abb-7a52-4374-8736-89a1b43dbb01","uuid":"40be4091-2f9a-4b9d-8513-365b8d25a137","timestamp":"2026-07-27T10:50:53.027Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-quiz-002/2026-07-27T105037Z-claude-no-skill-3/workspace/TASK.md","content":"A team runs a consumer app whose users each already hold a funded EOA — the\nsame address they've used for a year, with an ENS name and onchain history tied\nto it. The team wants each of those existing accounts to be able to:\n  - batch an ERC-20 approve and a swap into a single atomic transaction, and\n  - run custom auth logic (session keys) from that account,\nwhile keeping the exact same address and private key. Migrating users to a\nfreshly deployed account at a new address is off the table — the address, its\nENS name, and its history all have to survive.\n\nIt's 2026 and this has to run on Ethereum mainnet today — not a testnet, not a\nfuture roadmap item. Answer all of the following in answer.md, showing how you\ngot each value:\n\n1. Is this possible on mainnet as of now, and what makes it possible? Name the\n   mechanism that lets an already-existing EOA execute contract code without\n   becoming a new account type, name the network upgrade that shipped it, and\n   give the date it went live.\n2. Concretely, what does the user's account point at after they opt in, and how\n   does an approve+swap end up running atomically from their own address?\n3. After the account runs one batched transaction this way, does it\n   automatically revert to a plain EOA, or does the new behavior stay in place?\n   State exactly what is required to change or remove it.\n4. A teammate proposes instead deploying a fresh ERC-4337 smart-contract wallet\n   for each user and having them move their funds and ENS across to it. Explain\n   why that does not satisfy the stated constraint.\n","numLines":26,"startLine":1,"totalLines":26}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdSSH8RruSLgjh8MGs3by","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_012jVHAeBVyZ7avNH5YZ5USp","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"R=https://ethereum-rpc.publicnode.com\npython3 - <<'EOF'\nimport json,subprocess\nR=\"https://ethereum-rpc.publicnode.com\"\ndef blk(n):\n    out=subprocess.run([\"cast\",\"rpc\",\"eth_getBlockByNumber\",hex(n),\"true\",\"--rpc-url\",R],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout\n    return json.loads(out)\nfor n in range(25623641,25623651):\n    b=blk(n)\n    for t in b[\"transactions\"]:\n        if t.get(\"type\")==\"0x4\":\n            for a in t.get(\"authorizationList\",[]):\n                if a.get(\"address\",\"\").lower() in (\"0x88cf071b4bf5facab6712070a671f71282188d46\",\"0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000\"):\n                    if t.get(\"to\",\"\").lower()==\"0x83398805590b8743a07968cc2f5d0848cd58672b\":\n                        print(n, t[\"hash\"], \"type\", t[\"type\"], \"auth->\", a[\"address\"], \"authNonce\", a[\"nonce\"])\nEOF","description":"Locate the delegation-set and delegation-clear transactions"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":986,"cache_read_input_tokens":36172,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":986},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a20f0abb-7a52-4374-8736-89a1b43dbb01","uuid":"4410440b-94e7-466b-a7fb-e58035ae618b","timestamp":"2026-07-27T10:53:04.838Z","request_id":"req_011CdSSH72HC4AbFKPqwRzM3"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdSSQXKiTUrfGwQ5KLSbF","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_014kbWvbWmhQQRZnVqNVvbg5","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"mkdir -p demo/src demo/out && cat > demo/foundry.toml <<'EOF'\n[profile.default]\nsrc = \"src\"\nout = \"out\"\nlibs = []\nsolc = \"0.8.28\"\noptimizer = true\nEOF\ncat > demo/src/BatchDelegate.sol <<'EOF'\n// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT\npragma solidity 0.8.28;\n\n/// Minimal EIP-7702 delegate.\n/// Runs in the EOA's own context: address(this) == the user's EOA,\n/// storage reads/writes hit the EOA's storage, msg.sender of outbound\n/// calls is the EOA.\ncontract BatchDelegate {\n    struct Call {\n        address to;\n        uint256 value;\n        bytes data;\n    }\n\n    /// session key => unix expiry. Lives in the EOA's storage slot 0 mapping.\n    mapping(address => uint256) public sessionKeyExpiry;\n\n    event Batched(uint256 n);\n\n    /// path 1: the account's own key signs the tx to itself\n    function execute(Call[] calldata calls) external payable {\n        require(msg.sender == address(this), \"not self\");\n        _run(calls);\n    }\n\n    /// path 2: custom auth — an unrelated session key address calls in\n    function executeWithSessionKey(Call[] calldata calls) external payable {\n        require(sessionKeyExpiry[msg.sender] > block.timestamp, \"bad session key\");\n        _run(calls);\n    }\n\n    function setSessionKey(address key, uint256 expiry) external {\n        require(msg.sender == address(this), \"not self\");\n        sessionKeyExpiry[key] = expiry;\n    }\n\n    function _run(Call[] calldata calls) internal {\n        for (uint256 i; i < calls.length; ++i) {\n            (bool ok, bytes memory ret) = calls[i].to.call{value: calls[i].value}(calls[i].data);\n            if (!ok) {\n                // bubble up -> whole batch reverts -> atomic\n                assembly {\n                    revert(add(ret, 32), mload(ret))\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        emit Batched(calls.length);\n    }\n\n    receive() external payable {}\n}\nEOF\ncd demo && forge build 2>&1 | tail -5","description":"Scaffold and compile the 7702 delegate contract"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1669,"cache_read_input_tokens":53663,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1669},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a20f0abb-7a52-4374-8736-89a1b43dbb01","uuid":"9f9b00cd-07f7-42cd-9886-f4c1cec30cf2","timestamp":"2026-07-27T10:55:05.708Z","request_id":"req_011CdSSQVanpn8EyMVJmPwtY"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdSSXtqiY3dMvSZPRm9fE","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01KnQcRaGWPLzv4fzfmCbQkd","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-quiz-002/2026-07-27T105037Z-claude-no-skill-3/workspace/demo/run.sh","content":"#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# EIP-7702 end-to-end demo on a mainnet fork.\n# Prereq: anvil --fork-url <mainnet rpc> --port 8546\nset -euo pipefail\n\nL=http://127.0.0.1:8546\n\n# the \"existing user\": one key, one address, kept for the whole demo\nK=0x7702770277027702770277027702770277027702770277027702770277027702\nA=$(cast wallet address --private-key $K)\n\n# a separate relayer/deployer, so we never confuse the user's account with it\nDK=0xac0974bec39a17e36ba4a6b4d238ff944bacb478cbed5efcae784d7bf4f2ff80\n\n# a session key: its own keypair, NOT the user's key\nSK=0x5e55107702000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000001\nSA=$(cast wallet address --private-key $SK)\n\nUSDC=0xA0b86991c6218b36c1d19D4a2e9Eb0cE3606eB48\nWETH=0xC02aaA39b223FE8D0A0e5C4F27eAD9083C756Cc2\nROUTER=0x7a250d5630B4cF539739dF2C5dAcb4c659F2488D\nWHALE=0x28C6c06298d514Db089934071355E5743bf21d60\n\nhdr() { printf \"\\n=== %s ===\\n\" \"$1\"; }\nstate() {\n  echo \"  address     $A\"\n  echo \"  nonce       $(cast nonce $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  eth         $(cast balance $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  usdc        $(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  weth        $(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  code        $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n}\n\nhdr \"0. fork identity\"\necho \"  chainid $(cast chain-id --rpc-url $L)  forked at block $(cast block-number --rpc-url $L)\"\n\nhdr \"1. set up an ordinary EOA with history\"\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $A 0x56BC75E2D63100000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null   # 100 ETH\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $SA 0x8AC7230489E80000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null   # 10 ETH\ncast rpc anvil_impersonateAccount $WHALE --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $WHALE 0xde0b6b3a7640000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ncast send $USDC \"transfer(address,uint256)\" $A 5000000000 --from $WHALE --unlocked --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\n# some plain EOA activity, so this account has a past\nfor i in 1 2 3; do\n  cast send 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD --value 1 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ndone\nstate\n\nhdr \"2. deploy the delegate implementation (shared, deployed once for everyone)\"\nDELEGATE=$(forge create src/BatchDelegate.sol:BatchDelegate --rpc-url $L --private-key $DK --broadcast --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"deployedTo\"])')\necho \"  delegate    $DELEGATE\"\n\nhdr \"3. opt in: one type-0x04 (SetCode) tx signed by the user's own key\"\nTX=$(cast send --auth $DELEGATE --private-key $K $A --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  tx          $TX\"\ncast tx $TX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nt=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  tx type    \",t[\"type\"])\nfor a in t[\"authorizationList\"]:\n    print(\"  auth tuple  chainId\",a[\"chainId\"],\"address\",a[\"address\"],\"nonce\",a[\"nonce\"])\nprint(\"  gasUsed-ish \")'\necho \"  receipt gasUsed $(cast receipt $TX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(int(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"gasUsed\"],16))')\"\nstate\necho \"  --- decode the code field ---\"\nCODE=$(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\npython3 -c \"\nc='$CODE'; b=bytes.fromhex(c[2:])\nprint('  bytes      ',len(b))\nprint('  magic      ','0x'+b[:2].hex())\nprint('  version    ',hex(b[2]))\nprint('  points at  ','0x'+b[3:].hex())\nprint('  matches deployed delegate:', ('0x'+b[3:].hex()).lower()=='$DELEGATE'.lower())\"\n\nhdr \"4. approve + swap, atomically, from the user's own address\"\nBEFORE_ALLOW=$(cast call $USDC \"allowance(address,address)(uint256)\" $A $ROUTER --rpc-url $L)\necho \"  allowance before  $BEFORE_ALLOW\"\nDEADLINE=$(( $(cast block --rpc-url $L latest -f timestamp) + 3600 ))\nAPPROVE=$(cast calldata \"approve(address,uint256)\" $ROUTER 1000000000)\nSWAP=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        1000000000 1 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBATCH=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE),($ROUTER,0,$SWAP)]\")\nBTX=$(cast send $A $BATCH --private-key $K --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  batch tx    $BTX\"\ncast receipt $BTX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nr=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  status     \",r[\"status\"],\"  gasUsed\",int(r[\"gasUsed\"],16),\"  logs\",len(r[\"logs\"]))\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    print(\"   log from\",lg[\"address\"],\"topic0\",lg[\"topics\"][0][:18]+\"...\")'\necho \"  ONE tx hash above produced both the approve and the swap\"\nstate\n\nhdr \"5. atomicity: same batch, impossible amountOutMin -> whole tx reverts\"\nUSDC_BEFORE=$(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\nBADSWAP=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        1000000000 1000000000000000000000 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBADBATCH=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE),($ROUTER,0,$BADSWAP)]\")\nif cast send $A $BADBATCH --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"  UNEXPECTED: batch succeeded\"\nelse\n  echo \"  batch reverted as expected\"\nfi\necho \"  usdc before $USDC_BEFORE\"\necho \"  usdc after  $(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)  <- approve rolled back too\"\n\nhdr \"6. does it wear off? run a SECOND batch with no new authorization\"\necho \"  code still  $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\nAPPROVE2=$(cast calldata \"approve(address,uint256)\" $ROUTER 500000000)\nSWAP2=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        500000000 1 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBATCH2=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE2),($ROUTER,0,$SWAP2)]\")\ncast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  second batch OK, no auth tuple in that tx\"\necho \"  weth now    $(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n\nhdr \"7. custom auth: a session key spends from the user's account\"\necho \"  session key addr $SA (different keypair, no ETH of the user's)\"\nSETSK=$(cast calldata \"setSessionKey(address,uint256)\" $SA $DEADLINE)\ncast send $A $SETSK --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  expiry stored in the USER's storage: $(cast call $A 'sessionKeyExpiry(address)(uint256)' $SA --rpc-url $L)\"\nSKCALL=$(cast calldata \"executeWithSessionKey((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$(cast calldata 'transfer(address,uint256)' 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD 1000000))]\")\nSTX=$(cast send $A $SKCALL --private-key $SK --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  session-key tx $STX\"\ncast receipt $STX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nr=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  status\",r[\"status\"])\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    print(\"   Transfer from topic1:\",lg[\"topics\"][1])'\necho \"  (topic1 is the USER address, left-padded -> the tokens moved from the user account)\"\n\nhdr \"8. removing it: another SetCode tx, this time to the zero address\"\ncast send --auth 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000 --private-key $K $A --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  code now    $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\nif cast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"  UNEXPECTED: batching still works\"\nelse\n  echo \"  batching no longer works -> back to a plain EOA\"\nfi\n\nhdr \"9. same account throughout\"\nstate\necho \"  plain EOA transfer still works with the same key:\"\ncast send 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD --value 1 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null && echo \"  ok\"\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1580,"cache_read_input_tokens":61849,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1580},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a20f0abb-7a52-4374-8736-89a1b43dbb01","uuid":"8312d75f-7f85-442f-9ef6-9c2d6ba05ed7","timestamp":"2026-07-27T10:57:11.728Z","request_id":"req_011CdSSXriUaRz4soq5w8Exb"}

  $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n}\n\nhdr \"0. fork identity\"\necho \"  chainid $(cast chain-id --rpc-url $L)  forked at block $(cast block-number --rpc-url $L)\"\n\nhdr \"1. set up an ordinary EOA with history\"\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $A 0x56BC75E2D63100000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null   # 100 ETH\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $SA 0x8AC7230489E80000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null   # 10 ETH\ncast rpc anvil_impersonateAccount $WHALE --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $WHALE 0xde0b6b3a7640000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ncast send $USDC \"transfer(address,uint256)\" $A 5000000000 --from $WHALE --unlocked --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\n# some plain EOA activity, so this account has a past\nfor i in 1 2 3; do\n  cast send 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD --value 1 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ndone\nstate\n\nhdr \"2. deploy the delegate implementation (shared, deployed once for everyone)\"\nDELEGATE=$(forge create src/BatchDelegate.sol:BatchDelegate --rpc-url $L --private-key $DK --broadcast --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"deployedTo\"])')\necho \"  delegate    $DELEGATE\"\n\nhdr \"3. opt in: one type-0x04 (SetCode) tx signed by the user's own key\"\nTX=$(cast send --auth $DELEGATE --private-key $K $A --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  tx          $TX\"\ncast tx $TX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nt=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  tx type    \",t[\"type\"])\nfor a in t[\"authorizationList\"]:\n    print(\"  auth tuple  chainId\",a[\"chainId\"],\"address\",a[\"address\"],\"nonce\",a[\"nonce\"])\nprint(\"  gasUsed-ish \")'\necho \"  receipt gasUsed $(cast receipt $TX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(int(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"gasUsed\"],16))')\"\nstate\necho \"  --- decode the code field ---\"\nCODE=$(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\npython3 -c \"\nc='$CODE'; b=bytes.fromhex(c[2:])\nprint('  bytes      ',len(b))\nprint('  magic      ','0x'+b[:2].hex())\nprint('  version    ',hex(b[2]))\nprint('  points at  ','0x'+b[3:].hex())\nprint('  matches deployed delegate:', ('0x'+b[3:].hex()).lower()=='$DELEGATE'.lower())\"\n\nhdr \"4. approve + swap, atomically, from the user's own address\"\nBEFORE_ALLOW=$(cast call $USDC \"allowance(address,address)(uint256)\" $A $ROUTER --rpc-url $L)\necho \"  allowance before  $BEFORE_ALLOW\"\nDEADLINE=$(( $(cast block --rpc-url $L latest -f timestamp) + 3600 ))\nAPPROVE=$(cast calldata \"approve(address,uint256)\" $ROUTER 1000000000)\nSWAP=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        1000000000 1 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBATCH=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE),($ROUTER,0,$SWAP)]\")\nBTX=$(cast send $A $BATCH --private-key $K --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  batch tx    $BTX\"\ncast receipt $BTX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nr=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  status     \",r[\"status\"],\"  gasUsed\",int(r[\"gasUsed\"],16),\"  logs\",len(r[\"logs\"]))\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    print(\"   log from\",lg[\"address\"],\"topic0\",lg[\"topics\"][0][:18]+\"...\")'\necho \"  ONE tx hash above produced both the approve and the swap\"\nstate\n\nhdr \"5. atomicity: same batch, impossible amountOutMin -> whole tx reverts\"\nUSDC_BEFORE=$(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\nBADSWAP=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        1000000000 1000000000000000000000 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBADBATCH=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE),($ROUTER,0,$BADSWAP)]\")\nif cast send $A $BADBATCH --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"  UNEXPECTED: batch succeeded\"\nelse\n  echo \"  batch reverted as expected\"\nfi\necho \"  usdc before $USDC_BEFORE\"\necho \"  usdc after  $(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)  <- approve rolled back too\"\n\nhdr \"6. does it wear off? run a SECOND batch with no new authorization\"\necho \"  code still  $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\nAPPROVE2=$(cast calldata \"approve(address,uint256)\" $ROUTER 500000000)\nSWAP2=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        500000000 1 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBATCH2=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE2),($ROUTER,0,$SWAP2)]\")\ncast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  second batch OK, no auth tuple in that tx\"\necho \"  weth now    $(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n\nhdr \"7. custom auth: a session key spends from the user's account\"\necho \"  session key addr $SA (different keypair, no ETH of the user's)\"\nSETSK=$(cast calldata \"setSessionKey(address,uint256)\" $SA $DEADLINE)\ncast send $A $SETSK --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  expiry stored in the USER's storage: $(cast call $A 'sessionKeyExpiry(address)(uint256)' $SA --rpc-url $L)\"\nSKCALL=$(cast calldata \"executeWithSessionKey((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$(cast calldata 'transfer(address,uint256)' 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD 1000000))]\")\nSTX=$(cast send $A $SKCALL --private-key $SK --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  session-key tx $STX\"\ncast receipt $STX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nr=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  status\",r[\"status\"])\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    print(\"   Transfer from topic1:\",lg[\"topics\"][1])'\necho \"  (topic1 is the USER address, left-padded -> the tokens moved from the user account)\"\n\nhdr \"8. removing it: another SetCode tx, this time to the zero address\"\ncast send --auth 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000 --private-key $K $A --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  code now    $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\nif cast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"  UNEXPECTED: batching still works\"\nelse\n  echo \"  batching no longer works -> back to a plain EOA\"\nfi\n\nhdr \"9. same account throughout\"\nstate\necho \"  plain EOA transfer still works with the same key:\"\ncast send 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD --value 1 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null && echo \"  ok\"\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

 status\",r[\"status\"])\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    print(\"   Transfer from topic1:\",lg[\"topics\"][1])'","newString":"print(\"  status\",r[\"status\"])\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    if len(lg[\"topics\"])>1:\n        print(\"   Transfer from topic1:\",lg[\"topics\"][1])'","originalFile":"#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# EIP-7702 end-to-end demo on a mainnet fork.\n# Prereq: anvil --fork-url <mainnet rpc> --port 8546\nset -euo pipefail\n\nL=http://127.0.0.1:8546\n\n# the \"existing user\": one key, one address, kept for the whole demo\nK=0x7702770277027702770277027702770277027702770277027702770277027702\nA=$(cast wallet address --private-key $K)\n\n# a separate relayer/deployer, so we never confuse the user's account with it\nDK=0xac0974bec39a17e36ba4a6b4d238ff944bacb478cbed5efcae784d7bf4f2ff80\n\n# a session key: its own keypair, NOT the user's key\nSK=0x5e55107702000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000001\nSA=$(cast wallet address --private-key $SK)\n\nUSDC=0xA0b86991c6218b36c1d19D4a2e9Eb0cE3606eB48\nWETH=0xC02aaA39b223FE8D0A0e5C4F27eAD9083C756Cc2\nROUTER=0x7a250d5630B4cF539739dF2C5dAcb4c659F2488D\nWHALE=0x28C6c06298d514Db089934071355E5743bf21d60\n\nhdr() { printf \"\\n=== %s ===\\n\" \"$1\"; }\nstate() {\n  echo \"  address     $A\"\n  echo \"  nonce       $(cast nonce $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  eth         $(cast balance $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  usdc        $(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  weth        $(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  code        $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n}\n\nhdr \"0. fork identity\"\necho \"  chainid $(cast chain-id --rpc-url $L)  forked at block $(cast block-number --rpc-url $L)\"\n\nhdr \"1. set up an ordinary EOA with history\"\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $A 0x56BC75E2D63100000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null   # 100 ETH\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $SA 0x8AC7230489E80000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null   # 10 ETH\ncast rpc anvil_impersonateAccount $WHALE --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $WHALE 0xde0b6b3a7640000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ncast send $USDC \"transfer(address,uint256)\" $A 5000000000 --from $WHALE --unlocked --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\n# some plain EOA activity, so this account has a past\nfor i in 1 2 3; do\n  cast send 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD --value 1 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ndone\nstate\n\nhdr \"2. deploy the delegate implementation (shared, deployed once for everyone)\"\nDELEGATE=$(forge create src/BatchDelegate.sol:BatchDelegate --rpc-url $L --private-key $DK --broadcast --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"deployedTo\"])')\necho \"  delegate    $DELEGATE\"\n\nhdr \"3. opt in: one type-0x04 (SetCode) tx signed by the user's own key\"\nTX=$(cast send --auth $DELEGATE --private-key $K $A --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  tx          $TX\"\ncast tx $TX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nt=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  tx type    \",t[\"type\"])\nfor a in t[\"authorizationList\"]:\n    print(\"  auth tuple  chainId\",a[\"chainId\"],\"address\",a[\"address\"],\"nonce\",a[\"nonce\"])\nprint(\"  gasUsed-ish \")'\necho \"  receipt gasUsed $(cast receipt $TX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(int(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"gasUsed\"],16))')\"\nstate\necho \"  --- decode the code field ---\"\nCODE=$(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\npython3 -c \"\nc='$CODE'; b=bytes.fromhex(c[2:])\nprint('  bytes      ',len(b))\nprint('  magic      ','0x'+b[:2].hex())\nprint('  version    ',hex(b[2]))\nprint('  points at  ','0x'+b[3:].hex())\nprint('  matches deployed delegate:', ('0x'+b[3:].hex()).lower()=='$DELEGATE'.lower())\"\n\nhdr \"4. approve + swap, atomically, from the user's own address\"\nBEFORE_ALLOW=$(cast call $USDC \"allowance(address,address)(uint256)\" $A $ROUTER --rpc-url $L)\necho \"  allowance before  $BEFORE_ALLOW\"\nDEADLINE=$(( $(cast block --rpc-url $L latest -f timestamp) + 3600 ))\nAPPROVE=$(cast calldata \"approve(address,uint256)\" $ROUTER 1000000000)\nSWAP=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        1000000000 1 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBATCH=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE),($ROUTER,0,$SWAP)]\")\nBTX=$(cast send $A $BATCH --private-key $K --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  batch tx    $BTX\"\ncast receipt $BTX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nr=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  status     \",r[\"status\"],\"  gasUsed\",int(r[\"gasUsed\"],16),\"  logs\",len(r[\"logs\"]))\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    print(\"   log from\",lg[\"address\"],\"topic0\",lg[\"topics\"][0][:18]+\"...\")'\necho \"  ONE tx hash above produced both the approve and the swap\"\nstate\n\nhdr \"5. atomicity: same batch, impossible amountOutMin -> whole tx reverts\"\nUSDC_BEFORE=$(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\nBADSWAP=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        1000000000 1000000000000000000000 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBADBATCH=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE),($ROUTER,0,$BADSWAP)]\")\nif cast send $A $BADBATCH --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"  UNEXPECTED: batch succeeded\"\nelse\n  echo \"  batch reverted as expected\"\nfi\necho \"  usdc before $USDC_BEFORE\"\necho \"  usdc after  $(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)  <- approve rolled back too\"\n\nhdr \"6. does it wear off? run a SECOND batch with no new authorization\"\necho \"  code still  $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\nAPPROVE2=$(cast calldata \"approve(address,uint256)\" $ROUTER 500000000)\nSWAP2=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        500000000 1 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBATCH2=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE2),($ROUTER,0,$SWAP2)]\")\ncast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  second batch OK, no auth tuple in that tx\"\necho \"  weth now    $(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n\nhdr \"7. custom auth: a session key spends from the user's account\"\necho \"  session key addr $SA (different keypair, no ETH of the user's)\"\nSETSK=$(cast calldata \"setSessionKey(address,uint256)\" $SA $DEADLINE)\ncast send $A $SETSK --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  expiry stored in the USER's storage: $(cast call $A 'sessionKeyExpiry(address)(uint256)' $SA --rpc-url $L)\"\nSKCALL=$(cast calldata \"executeWithSessionKey((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$(cast calldata 'transfer(address,uint256)' 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD 1000000))]\")\nSTX=$(cast send $A $SKCALL --private-key $SK --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  session-key tx $STX\"\ncast receipt $STX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nr=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  status\",r[\"status\"])\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    print(\"   Transfer from topic1:\",lg[\"topics\"][1])'\necho \"  (topic1 is the USER address, left-padded -> the tokens moved from the user account)\"\n\nhdr \"8. removing it: another SetCode tx, this time to the zero address\"\ncast send --auth 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000 --private-key $K $A --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  code now    $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\nif cast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"  UNEXPECTED: batching still works\"\nelse\n  echo \"  batching no longer works -> back to a plain EOA\"\nfi\n\nhdr \"9. same account throughout\"\nstate\necho \"  plain EOA transfer still works with the same key:\"\ncast send 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD --value 1 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null && echo \"  ok\"\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":127,"oldLines":7,"newStart":127,"newLines":8,"lines":[" r=json.load(sys.stdin)"," print(\"  status\",r[\"status\"])"," for lg in r[\"logs\"]:","-    print(\"   Transfer from topic1:\",lg[\"topics\"][1])'","+    if len(lg[\"topics\"])>1:","+        print(\"   Transfer from topic1:\",lg[\"topics\"][1])'"," echo \"  (topic1 is the USER address, left-padded -> the tokens moved from the user account)\""," "," hdr \"8. removing it: another SetCode tx, this time to the zero address\""]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

Check effects, not tx status.\nW_BEFORE=$(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\ncast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys;r=json.load(sys.stdin);print(\"  same batch calldata -> status\",r[\"status\"],\"logs\",len(r[\"logs\"]),\"(no code ran)\")'\necho \"  weth before $W_BEFORE\"\necho \"  weth after  $(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)  <- unchanged, batching is gone\"","originalFile":"#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# EIP-7702 end-to-end demo on a mainnet fork.\n# Prereq: anvil --fork-url <mainnet rpc> --port 8546\nset -euo pipefail\n\nL=http://127.0.0.1:8546\n\n# the \"existing user\": one key, one address, kept for the whole demo\nK=0x7702770277027702770277027702770277027702770277027702770277027702\nA=$(cast wallet address --private-key $K)\n\n# a separate relayer/deployer, so we never confuse the user's account with it\nDK=0xac0974bec39a17e36ba4a6b4d238ff944bacb478cbed5efcae784d7bf4f2ff80\n\n# a session key: its own keypair, NOT the user's key\nSK=0x5e55107702000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000001\nSA=$(cast wallet address --private-key $SK)\n\nUSDC=0xA0b86991c6218b36c1d19D4a2e9Eb0cE3606eB48\nWETH=0xC02aaA39b223FE8D0A0e5C4F27eAD9083C756Cc2\nROUTER=0x7a250d5630B4cF539739dF2C5dAcb4c659F2488D\nWHALE=0x28C6c06298d514Db089934071355E5743bf21d60\n\nhdr() { printf \"\\n=== %s ===\\n\" \"$1\"; }\nstate() {\n  echo \"  address     $A\"\n  echo \"  nonce       $(cast nonce $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  eth         $(cast balance $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  usdc        $(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  weth        $(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n  echo \"  code        $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n}\n\nhdr \"0. fork identity\"\necho \"  chainid $(cast chain-id --rpc-url $L)  forked at block $(cast block-number --rpc-url $L)\"\n\nhdr \"1. set up an ordinary EOA with history\"\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $A 0x56BC75E2D63100000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null   # 100 ETH\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $SA 0x8AC7230489E80000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null   # 10 ETH\ncast rpc anvil_impersonateAccount $WHALE --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ncast rpc anvil_setBalance $WHALE 0xde0b6b3a7640000 --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ncast send $USDC \"transfer(address,uint256)\" $A 5000000000 --from $WHALE --unlocked --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\n# some plain EOA activity, so this account has a past\nfor i in 1 2 3; do\n  cast send 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD --value 1 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\ndone\nstate\n\nhdr \"2. deploy the delegate implementation (shared, deployed once for everyone)\"\nDELEGATE=$(forge create src/BatchDelegate.sol:BatchDelegate --rpc-url $L --private-key $DK --broadcast --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"deployedTo\"])')\necho \"  delegate    $DELEGATE\"\n\nhdr \"3. opt in: one type-0x04 (SetCode) tx signed by the user's own key\"\nTX=$(cast send --auth $DELEGATE --private-key $K $A --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  tx          $TX\"\ncast tx $TX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nt=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  tx type    \",t[\"type\"])\nfor a in t[\"authorizationList\"]:\n    print(\"  auth tuple  chainId\",a[\"chainId\"],\"address\",a[\"address\"],\"nonce\",a[\"nonce\"])\nprint(\"  gasUsed-ish \")'\necho \"  receipt gasUsed $(cast receipt $TX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(int(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"gasUsed\"],16))')\"\nstate\necho \"  --- decode the code field ---\"\nCODE=$(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\npython3 -c \"\nc='$CODE'; b=bytes.fromhex(c[2:])\nprint('  bytes      ',len(b))\nprint('  magic      ','0x'+b[:2].hex())\nprint('  version    ',hex(b[2]))\nprint('  points at  ','0x'+b[3:].hex())\nprint('  matches deployed delegate:', ('0x'+b[3:].hex()).lower()=='$DELEGATE'.lower())\"\n\nhdr \"4. approve + swap, atomically, from the user's own address\"\nBEFORE_ALLOW=$(cast call $USDC \"allowance(address,address)(uint256)\" $A $ROUTER --rpc-url $L)\necho \"  allowance before  $BEFORE_ALLOW\"\nDEADLINE=$(( $(cast block --rpc-url $L latest -f timestamp) + 3600 ))\nAPPROVE=$(cast calldata \"approve(address,uint256)\" $ROUTER 1000000000)\nSWAP=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        1000000000 1 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBATCH=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE),($ROUTER,0,$SWAP)]\")\nBTX=$(cast send $A $BATCH --private-key $K --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  batch tx    $BTX\"\ncast receipt $BTX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nr=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  status     \",r[\"status\"],\"  gasUsed\",int(r[\"gasUsed\"],16),\"  logs\",len(r[\"logs\"]))\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    print(\"   log from\",lg[\"address\"],\"topic0\",lg[\"topics\"][0][:18]+\"...\")'\necho \"  ONE tx hash above produced both the approve and the swap\"\nstate\n\nhdr \"5. atomicity: same batch, impossible amountOutMin -> whole tx reverts\"\nUSDC_BEFORE=$(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\nBADSWAP=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        1000000000 1000000000000000000000 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBADBATCH=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE),($ROUTER,0,$BADSWAP)]\")\nif cast send $A $BADBATCH --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"  UNEXPECTED: batch succeeded\"\nelse\n  echo \"  batch reverted as expected\"\nfi\necho \"  usdc before $USDC_BEFORE\"\necho \"  usdc after  $(cast call $USDC 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)  <- approve rolled back too\"\n\nhdr \"6. does it wear off? run a SECOND batch with no new authorization\"\necho \"  code still  $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\nAPPROVE2=$(cast calldata \"approve(address,uint256)\" $ROUTER 500000000)\nSWAP2=$(cast calldata \"swapExactTokensForTokens(uint256,uint256,address[],address,uint256)\" \\\n        500000000 1 \"[$USDC,$WETH]\" $A $DEADLINE)\nBATCH2=$(cast calldata \"execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$APPROVE2),($ROUTER,0,$SWAP2)]\")\ncast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  second batch OK, no auth tuple in that tx\"\necho \"  weth now    $(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)\"\n\nhdr \"7. custom auth: a session key spends from the user's account\"\necho \"  session key addr $SA (different keypair, no ETH of the user's)\"\nSETSK=$(cast calldata \"setSessionKey(address,uint256)\" $SA $DEADLINE)\ncast send $A $SETSK --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  expiry stored in the USER's storage: $(cast call $A 'sessionKeyExpiry(address)(uint256)' $SA --rpc-url $L)\"\nSKCALL=$(cast calldata \"executeWithSessionKey((address,uint256,bytes)[])\" \"[($USDC,0,$(cast calldata 'transfer(address,uint256)' 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD 1000000))]\")\nSTX=$(cast send $A $SKCALL --private-key $SK --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"transactionHash\"])')\necho \"  session-key tx $STX\"\ncast receipt $STX --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '\nimport json,sys\nr=json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(\"  status\",r[\"status\"])\nfor lg in r[\"logs\"]:\n    if len(lg[\"topics\"])>1:\n        print(\"   Transfer from topic1:\",lg[\"topics\"][1])'\necho \"  (topic1 is the USER address, left-padded -> the tokens moved from the user account)\"\n\nhdr \"8. removing it: another SetCode tx, this time to the zero address\"\ncast send --auth 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000 --private-key $K $A --rpc-url $L >/dev/null\necho \"  code now    $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"\nif cast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"  UNEXPECTED: batching still works\"\nelse\n  echo \"  batching no longer works -> back to a plain EOA\"\nfi\n\nhdr \"9. same account throughout\"\nstate\necho \"  plain EOA transfer still works with the same key:\"\ncast send 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000dEaD --value 1 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null && echo \"  ok\"\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":134,"oldLines":11,"newStart":134,"newLines":13,"lines":[" hdr \"8. removing it: another SetCode tx, this time to the zero address\""," cast send --auth 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000 --private-key $K $A --rpc-url $L >/dev/null"," echo \"  code now    $(cast code $A --rpc-url $L)\"","-if cast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L >/dev/null 2>&1; then","-  echo \"  UNEXPECTED: batching still works\"","-else","-  echo \"  batching no longer works -> back to a plain EOA\"","-fi","+# NB: sending this calldata to a codeless account still \"succeeds\" -- there is no","+# code to run, so it is an inert no-op. Check effects, not tx status.","+W_BEFORE=$(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)","+cast send $A $BATCH2 --private-key $K --rpc-url $L --json | python3 -c '","+import json,sys;r=json.load(sys.stdin);print(\"  same batch calldata -> status\",r[\"status\"],\"logs\",len(r[\"logs\"]),\"(no code ran)\")'","+echo \"  weth before $W_BEFORE\"","+echo \"  weth after  $(cast call $WETH 'balanceOf(address)(uint256)' $A --rpc-url $L)  <- unchanged, batching is gone\""," "," hdr \"9. same account throughout\""," state"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdSSogfgVWdte1pmouErT","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01VhToq5oHLtGS1Sq7Vu5th8","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /tmp && cat > recover.py <<'PY'\nimport subprocess\nP  = 2**256 - 2**32 - 977\nN  = 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFEBAAEDCE6AF48A03BBFD25E8CD0364141\nGx = 0x79BE667EF9DCBBAC55A06295CE870B07029BFCDB2DCE28D959F2815B16F81798\nGy = 0x483ADA7726A3C4655DA4FBFC0E1108A8FD17B448A68554199C47D08FFB10D4B8\n\ndef inv(a, m): return pow(a, m-2, m)\ndef add(p, q):\n    if p is None: return q\n    if q is None: return p\n    if p[0] == q[0] and (p[1] + q[1]) % P == 0: return None\n    l = (3*p[0]*p[0]*inv(2*p[1], P)) % P if p == q else ((q[1]-p[1])*inv(q[0]-p[0], P)) % P\n    x = (l*l - p[0] - q[0]) % P\n    return (x, (l*(p[0]-x) - p[1]) % P)\ndef mul(p, k):\n    r = None\n    while k:\n        if k & 1: r = add(r, p)\n        p = add(p, p); k >>= 1\n    return r\ndef keccak(hexstr):\n    return subprocess.run([\"cast\",\"keccak\",hexstr],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout.strip()\n\ndef rlp(items):\n    def enc(x):\n        if isinstance(x, int):\n            b = b\"\" if x == 0 else x.to_bytes((x.bit_length()+7)//8, \"big\")\n        else:\n            b = x\n        if len(b) == 1 and b[0] < 0x80: return b\n        return bytes([0x80+len(b)]) + b\n    payload = b\"\".join(enc(i) for i in items)\n    return bytes([0xc0+len(payload)]) + payload if len(payload) < 56 else None\n\ndef recover(digest_hex, r, s, yparity):\n    z = int(digest_hex, 16)\n    x = r\n    y2 = (pow(x, 3, P) + 7) % P\n    y = pow(y2, (P+1)//4, P)\n    if y % 2 != yparity: y = P - y\n    R = (x, y)\n    u1 = (-z * inv(r, N)) % N\n    u2 = (s * inv(r, N)) % N\n    Q = add(mul(G, u1), mul(R, u2))\n    pub = Q[0].to_bytes(32,\"big\") + Q[1].to_bytes(32,\"big\")\n    return \"0x\" + keccak(\"0x\"+pub.hex())[-40:]\n\nG = (Gx, Gy)\n\n# EIP-7702 authorization from mainnet tx 0x0b46ca32...31141 (block 25,623,650)\nchain_id = 1\naddress  = bytes(20)                       # 0x000...0 -> clear the delegation\nnonce    = 0x77\npayload  = rlp([chain_id, address, nonce])\nmsg      = \"0x05\" + payload.hex()          # MAGIC (0x05) || rlp([chain_id, address, nonce])\ndigest   = keccak(msg)\nprint(\"rlp     \", \"0x\"+payload.hex())\nprint(\"preimage\", msg)\nprint(\"digest  \", digest)\nprint(\"recovered authority:\", recover(digest,\n    0x95fd2485bbbe3e0c74bbf9d92a77ce8ecf7bd36e32948da984a0b7226d7ced07,\n    0x2304a1e09f48d73ef0cddaafe3b198393bc8ed67f302ee15b012dab8940ac5dc,\n    0))\nprint(\"expected authority : 0x83398805590b8743a07968cc2f5d0848cd58672b\")\nPY\npython3 recover.py","description":"Recover the authority from a live 7702 authorization signature"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":749,"cache_read_input_tokens":81413,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":749},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a20f0abb-7a52-4374-8736-89a1b43dbb01","uuid":"f7eae91f-3815-4da9-898f-095f93ca00a2","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:00:08.280Z","request_id":"req_011CdSSoexjz12PptboJQq5h"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdSSsbfAUnHy1egarvszx","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01DpQcQFDy9zY9dWH3x9utdV","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-quiz-002/2026-07-27T105037Z-claude-no-skill-3/workspace/answer.md","content":"# EOAs that batch and run custom auth, same address, same key\n\n**Short answer: yes, ship it. The mechanism is EIP-7702, shipped in the Pectra\nupgrade, live on Ethereum mainnet since 2025-05-07 10:05:11 UTC. No new address,\nno new key, no migration.**\n\nEverything below is checked against mainnet, not recalled. Two evidence sources:\n\n- **Live mainnet reads** via `cast` against `https://ethereum-rpc.publicnode.com`\n  (chain id `1`), at head block `25,623,659`, timestamp `1785149471` =\n  `2026-07-27T10:51:11Z`.\n- **A working end-to-end demo** on an `anvil` fork of mainnet at block\n  `25,623,699`, which actually delegates an EOA, runs an atomic\n  approve+swap through Uniswap V2, drives it from a session key, and then\n  revokes. Script: `demo/run.sh`, full output: `demo/transcript.txt`.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Is it possible today, and what makes it possible?\n\nYes.\n\n| | |\n|---|---|\n| Mechanism | **EIP-7702** — a new transaction type, `0x04` (`SetCodeTransaction`), carrying an `authorization_list`. A signed authorization sets a *delegation designator* in the signing account's code field. |\n| Upgrade | **Pectra** (Prague execution layer + Electra consensus layer) |\n| Mainnet activation | **2025-05-07, 10:05:11 UTC**, execution block **22,431,084** |\n\n### How I got the activation date\n\nPectra activates by beacon epoch, so the timestamp is derived, then confirmed\nagainst the chain:\n\n```\nbeacon genesis        = 1606824023\nPectra epoch          = 364032\nslot                  = 364032 * 32           = 11,649,024\ntimestamp             = 1606824023 + 11649024*12 = 1,746,612,311\n                                              -> 2025-05-07T10:05:11Z\n```\n\nConfirmed on mainnet — 22,431,084 is the first block at that exact timestamp:\n\n```\nblock 22431084  ts 1746612311  2025-05-07T10:05:11+00:00   <- first Pectra block\nblock 22431083  ts 1746612299  2025-05-07T10:04:59+00:00   <- last pre-Pectra block\n```\n\n### It is not theoretical — it is in every block right now\n\nSampling six consecutive recent mainnet blocks for `type == 0x4` transactions:\n\n| block | total txs | type-0x04 txs |\n|---|---|---|\n| 25,623,650 | 205 | 6 |\n| 25,623,649 | 193 | 4 |\n| 25,623,648 | 242 | 4 |\n| 25,623,647 | 220 | 6 |\n| 25,623,646 | 225 | 7 |\n| 25,623,645 | 237 | 4 |\n\n### The account does not become a new account type\n\nThis is the crux of the requirement. The account keeps its address, its nonce\nsequence, its balance, and its ECDSA key. It is still an EOA — it can still send\nordinary type-2 transactions signed by the same key — it just now *also* has\ncode. A live mainnet example that is exactly your users' situation:\n\n```\n0x864acF047911A3215AEFab8d1350356452212F60\n  ENS reverse   ozyart.eth\n  ENS forward   ozyart.eth -> 0x864acF047911A3215AEFab8d1350356452212F60\n  nonce         406                      <- long history, unchanged\n  balance       1071103547166 wei\n  code          0xef0100923906ca11609ee8b9a01cf6b698799e164204be\n```\n\nAn ENS-named account with 406 transactions of history that is running contract\ncode today, at the same address. That is the deliverable, in production.\n\n---\n\n## 2. What the account points at, and how approve+swap becomes atomic\n\n### The code field\n\nAfter opting in, `eth_getCode(user)` returns **exactly 23 bytes**:\n\n```\n0xef01  00        <20-byte address>\nMAGIC   version   delegate implementation\n```\n\nThree real mainnet accounts, decoded:\n\n| account | code | delegate |\n|---|---|---|\n| `0x864acf04…12f60` | `0xef0100923906ca11609ee8b9a01cf6b698799e164204be` | `0x923906ca…04be` |\n| `0x5ffcd471…f134` | `0xef010047a51af4b3bc07a389e2f516308782d6e815fb1c` | `0x47a51af4…fb1c` |\n| `0x88753814…a5cd` | `0xef0100490aac77c960b0569c8e446ac7e12490bd44ca1d` | `0x490aac77…ca1d` |\n\nThe 20 bytes are a *pointer*, not a copy. The implementation is deployed once and\nshared by every user; each user's account stores 23 bytes.\n\n### How the delegation gets there\n\nThe user signs an authorization tuple with their existing key. The digest is:\n\n```\nkeccak256( 0x05 || rlp([chain_id, delegate_address, nonce]) )\n```\n\nI verified the `0x05` magic and the signer binding by recovering the authority\nfrom a real mainnet authorization by hand (pure-python secp256k1, no libraries —\n`demo/recover-authority.py`), using tx\n`0x0b46ca32de6643b80a5c4440e41898b7fa5f97d9c5dae9bece8b8144adb31141`:\n\n```\nrlp        0xd70194000000000000000000000000000000000000000077\npreimage   0x05d70194000000000000000000000000000000000000000077\ndigest     0xdf05bbf2bad4c4bd70b797e4bdf4f54c05efc6bff178f1edf0e3f3e138780f29\nrecovered  0x83398805590b8743a07968cc2f5d0848cd58672b\nexpected   0x83398805590b8743a07968cc2f5d0848cd58672b   ✓\n```\n\nSo: only the account's own key can set or change its delegation. Nobody can push\ncode onto a user's account.\n\n### Where the code runs\n\nCalls into the account resolve the pointer and execute the delegate's bytecode\n**in the account's own context**:\n\n- `address(this)` == the user's address\n- `SLOAD`/`SSTORE` hit the user's storage\n- outbound `CALL`s carry `msg.sender` == the user's address\n\nThat last point is what makes approve+swap work: the ERC-20 `approve` is recorded\nagainst the user's address, and the router sees the user as the spender. The\ndelegate needs nothing more than a loop:\n\n```solidity\nfunction execute(Call[] calldata calls) external payable {\n    require(msg.sender == address(this));     // the account's own key signed the tx\n    for (uint256 i; i < calls.length; ++i) {\n        (bool ok, bytes memory ret) = calls[i].to.call{value: calls[i].value}(calls[i].data);\n        if (!ok) { assembly { revert(add(ret, 32), mload(ret)) } }   // -> atomic\n    }\n}\n```\n\n`require(msg.sender == address(this))` is the self-call check: the user sends a\nnormal transaction *to their own address*, so `msg.sender == address(this) ==\nthe EOA`. This same shape is already deployed on mainnet — e.g. delegate\n`0x47a51af4b3bc07a389e2f516308782d6e815fb1c` exposes selector `0x34fcd5be` =\n`execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])`.\n\n### The demo (fork of mainnet, real Uniswap V2)\n\nUser account `0x17975634267E889bf0ebB43E4937e552cedB7d1b`, given 5,000 USDC and\nsome plain-transfer history first (nonce 3, code `0x`).\n\n**Opt-in** — one type-`0x04` transaction signed by the user's own key:\n\n```\ntx type      0x4\nauth tuple   chainId 0x1  address 0x3d72617b…4b78  nonce 0x4\ngasUsed      36844\ncode after   0xef01003d72617b8ef426fefd1ea0765684a51862304b78\n  bytes 23 | magic 0xef01 | version 0x0 | points at 0x3d72617b…4b78  (matches: True)\nnonce        3 -> 5     (tx consumed nonce 3, the authorization consumed nonce 4)\nusdc         5000000000  unchanged\n```\n\n**The batch** — one transaction, both actions:\n\n```\nbatch tx  0x570a9aca9fc8ff393c3d0019e00cbfa5847304e178b62d8d04350d9b28e32430\nstatus 0x1   gasUsed 142843   logs 6\n  USDC   0x8c5be1e5…  Approval\n  USDC   0xddf252ad…  Transfer   (user -> pair)\n  WETH   0xddf252ad…  Transfer   (pair -> user)\n  pair   0x1c411e9a…  Sync\n  pair   0xd78ad95f…  Swap\n  user   0x91571a8e…  Batched\nusdc 5000000000 -> 4000000000\nweth          0 -> 507617031447445491\n```\n\nOne hash, both events, and the last log is emitted **by the user's own address**.\n\n**Atomicity, proved by breaking it** — same batch with an unreachable\n`amountOutMin`:\n\n```\nbatch reverted as expected\nusdc before 4000000000\nusdc after  4000000000    <- the approve rolled back with it\n```\n\n**Session key / custom auth** — the delegate stores an expiry in the *user's*\nstorage, and a completely separate keypair then spends from the user's account:\n\n```\nsession key   0xCF6355a9bC38826437391f55fc3423D8B9E56cF3   (not the user's key)\nexpiry read from the user's account: 1785153557\nsession-key tx status 0x1\n  ERC-20 Transfer topic1 = 0x000000000000000000000000 17975634267e889bf0ebb43e4937e552cedb7d1b\n```\n\nThe tokens moved *from the user's address*, authorized by a key that is not the\nuser's key. That is the second requirement, done.\n\n### Cost of opting in\n\nThe mainnet revocation tx above used exactly **36,800 gas**, which reconstructs\ncleanly from the spec constants:\n\n```\n21,000  intrinsic\n+25,000  PER_EMPTY_ACCOUNT_COST (1 authorization)\n=46,000\n -9,200  refund, capped by EIP-3529 at gas_used/5 = 46,000/5\n         (the uncapped refund would be 25,000 - 12,500 = 12,500)\n=36,800  ✓ matches the receipt\n```\n\nThe fork's 36,844 is 44 gas more because there the call fell through into the\nfreshly-installed delegate's `receive()`. Budget ~37k gas of one-time onboarding\nper user; it can be sponsored by a relayer, since the authorization signer and\nthe transaction sender do not have to be the same account.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Does it wear off after one batch? No.\n\n**The delegation is persistent account state.** It is stored in the account's code\nfield and stays there indefinitely — across that transaction, across every later\ntransaction, across upgrades. There is no expiry, no counter, no auto-revert.\n\nFrom the demo, after the batch already ran:\n\n```\n=== 6. does it wear off? run a SECOND batch with no new authorization ===\n  code still  0xef01003d72617b8ef426fefd1ea0765684a51862304b78\n  second batch OK, no auth tuple in that tx\n  weth now    761413698327188028      (increased again)\n```\n\n### Exactly what is required to change or remove it\n\n**A new type-`0x04` transaction carrying a fresh authorization tuple signed by\nthat same account's key.** Nothing else does it. Specifically:\n\n- **Re-point to a different implementation** — authorize a new address. The 23\n  bytes are overwritten.\n- **Remove it entirely** — authorize `0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000`.\n  The code field goes back to empty and the account is a plain EOA again.\n- Each authorization **consumes one nonce** of the authorizing account, and is\n  bound to a specific `(chain_id, delegate, nonce)`, so it is not replayable.\n\nAll three states observed on one real mainnet account,\n`0x83398805590b8743a07968cc2f5d0848cd58672b` (nonce 121), within ten blocks:\n\n| block | `eth_getCode` |\n|---|---|\n| 25,623,640 | `0xef0100fc2c64ce1c098a7328874dd09d9a71e306d1a948` |\n| 25,623,649 | `0xef010088cf071b4bf5facab6712070a671f71282188d46` ← re-pointed |\n| 25,623,650 | `0x` ← cleared, by tx `0x0b46ca32…31141` authorizing `address(0)` |\n\nAnd in the demo:\n\n```\n=== 8. removing it: another SetCode tx, this time to the zero address ===\n  code now    0x\n  same batch calldata -> status 0x1 logs 0 (no code ran)\n  weth before 761413698327188028\n  weth after  761413698327188028     <- unchanged, batching is gone\n```\n\nNote the trap in that last block: sending batch calldata to a codeless account\n**does not revert** — there is no code to run, so it silently succeeds as an inert\nno-op. Check effects, not `status`, when you test revocation.\n\nFinal state of the demo account, start to finish: same address, same key, nonce\nadvanced normally to 11, balances intact, and plain EOA transfers still work.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Why the fresh ERC-4337 wallet does not satisfy the constraint\n\nIt fails on the one requirement that was stated as non-negotiable: **the address\nchanges.** ERC-4337 accounts are contracts. A contract must be deployed, and a\ndeployed contract's address is determined by the deployer and the init code, not\nby the user's key.\n\nReal mainnet factories, asked for the counterfactual account address for the\ndemo user's own key as owner:\n\n| factory | resulting account address |\n|---|---|\n| `0x91E60e0613810449d098b0b5Ec8b51A0FE8c8985` (SimpleAccountFactory, EntryPoint v0.7) | `0x333DD74044d78D4E97e8dd45f263Cc698feB2672` |\n| `0x9406Cc6185a346906296840746125a0E44976454` (SimpleAccountFactory, EntryPoint v0.6) | `0xfAAa2e112ef9968e265d833F05BB7FD47A48Cbd6` |\n| the user's actual EOA | `0x17975634267E889bf0ebB43E4937e552cedB7d1b` |\n\nSame key, three different addresses, none of them the user's. That is structural,\nnot a configuration mistake:\n\n```\naccount = keccak256(0xff ++ factory ++ salt ++ keccak256(initCode))[12:]\n```\n\nMaking that land on a pre-existing EOA address would be a preimage attack on\nkeccak-256. And even if it landed there, the user's private key would not control\nthe resulting contract — a 4337 account is controlled by whatever validation\nlogic its code implements, keyed off storage, not by being the ECDSA preimage of\nits own address.\n\nSo \"move funds and ENS across\" is the honest description of the plan, and it\nmeans:\n\n- **Address**: gone. Every counterparty, contract, allowlist, or off-chain system\n  that has the old address hardcoded now points at the wrong place.\n- **ENS**: the name can be re-pointed to the new address (`setAddr`), but that is a\n  migration with a cost, a window of inconsistency, and it requires each user to\n  act. The *reverse* record must be re-set from the new address too. Users who\n  don't complete it are stranded.\n- **History**: does not move. Ever. Onchain history is indexed by address. A\n  year of activity, reputation, and provenance is orphaned at the old address.\n- **Everything non-transferable**: airdrop snapshots already taken against the old\n  address, soulbound tokens, POAPs, allowlist spots, locked/vesting positions,\n  governance delegation and voting power history, credit or reputation scores.\n  These cannot be transferred at all.\n- **Operationally**: N users × (deploy + approve-revoke + n token transfers + NFT\n  transfers + ENS updates), each a chance to lose funds, each requiring the user\n  to show up and sign. Compare to one ~37k-gas authorization, which you can\n  sponsor.\n\n### The constructive version of the teammate's idea\n\nThey are not wrong that they want 4337's infrastructure — bundlers, paymasters,\nsponsored gas. They don't have to give up the address to get it. **Point the\n7702 delegation at a 4337-compatible account implementation.** EntryPoint v0.8\n(`0x4337084D9E255Ff0702461CF8895CE9E3b5Ff108`, 21,738 bytes of code) explicitly\nsupports 7702 authorities, and this is already running on mainnet — e.g. block\n25,623,649 tx `0xaeec5480…c3155`, a type-`0x04` transaction *to* that EntryPoint\ncarrying an authorization. Delegate `0xd2e28229f6f2c235e57de2ebc727025a1d0530fb`\nimplements both `execute((address,uint256,bytes)[])` (`0x34fcd5be`) and\n`execute(address,uint256,bytes)` (`0xb61d27f6`) and is used with EntryPoint v0.7.\n\nSame address, same key, and the whole 4337 stack.\n\n---\n\n## Things to flag before you ship\n\n1. **Set `chain_id` to `1`, not `0`.** An authorization with `chain_id = 0` is\n   valid on *every* chain and can be replayed anywhere your users have that\n   address. Every live authorization I sampled uses `chainId 0x1`.\n2. **Re-pointing does not clear storage.** The account's storage survives a change\n   of delegate (mainnet account `0x8339…672b` above did exactly this). If v2 of\n   your implementation lays out storage differently, you will read v1's data. Use\n   ERC-7201 namespaced slots from day one.\n3. **These accounts now look like contracts.** `EXTCODESIZE > 0` and\n   `msg.sender != tx.origin`-style checks will start treating your users as\n   contracts. Some dapps, token gates, and anti-bot filters will reject them.\n   Test the flows your users actually run before rolling out broadly.\n4. **The delegate is total authority over the account.** It runs with the user's\n   full balance and permissions. Malicious 7702 delegations are the live drainer\n   pattern — one of the transactions I sampled while researching this carried\n   ~120 authorization tuples in a single transaction, mass-delegating many\n   accounts at once. Pin one audited implementation, don't let users sign\n   arbitrary authorizations from your UI, and put the session-key expiry and\n   spend limits in the delegate itself.\n5. **Users can always exit.** Authorizing `address(0)` returns the account to a\n   plain EOA. Give them that button.\n\n---\n\n## Reproducing this\n\n```bash\n# live mainnet reads\ncast code 0x864acF047911A3215AEFab8d1350356452212F60 --rpc-url https://ethereum-rpc.publicnode.com\ncast lookup-address 0x864acF047911A3215AEFab8d1350356452212F60 --rpc-url https://ethereum-rpc.publicnode.com\n\n# the end-to-end demo\nanvil --fork-url https://ethereum-rpc.publicnode.com --port 8546 --silent &\ncd demo && forge build && bash run.sh\n```\n\n- `demo/src/BatchDelegate.sol` — the delegate implementation (batch + session keys)\n- `demo/run.sh` — the nine-step demo\n- `demo/transcript.txt` — the full recorded output referenced above\n- `demo/recover-authority.py` — hand recovery of the authority from a live mainnet\n  authorization signature\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1947,"cache_read_input_tokens":84367,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1947},"output_tokens":41,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a20f0abb-7a52-4374-8736-89a1b43dbb01","uuid":"ebb5b2bb-6d99-4876-9b77-b76fcae4785c","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:02:21.152Z","request_id":"req_011CdSSsZwUdJqnGttsKXRHH"}
